04 May 2026 19:56

1. Main Branch - fix E-STOP on ESC reset

CY 1. Run can_sniffer.py first. Confirm "TESLA CHASSIS CAN CONFIRMED" and turn the wheel by hand to verify the angle decode tracks. This proves
the bus is alive AND that 0x370 is being read correctly.
CY 2. Run move.py 0. Just engage at center. Wheel should not move. eacStatus should transition to ACTIVE. If it does, the protocol is healthy.
3. Run move.py 5, then move.py -5, then move.py 0. Confirm sign convention matches what Jordan saw on 03 May (positive = right).
move 5: twitching, move -5: twitching to +2 deg then not moving, move 0: twitching, after 10 sec jerks to 0 deg
4. Use steer.py for live control or tesla_steering_test.py for slider control.
Constant flickering Available / Active, after 15 sec switches to flickering Available / Inhibited, after 15 sec holds on Available.
After about 30 sec of having the program open, Error reads HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ
All of the above is with no input given / no angle commanded
Wheel steering graphic: rotation angle is flipped, also errors are not logged
Sometimes it gets in a state where EAC = AVAILABLE and ERROR = NONE,
sometimes it gets in a state where EAC = flickering AVAILABLE / ACTIVE and ERROR = flickering NONE / HIGH_ANNGLE_RATE_REQ
Sometimes it gets in a state where EAC = AVAILABLE or INHIBITED constantly
Furthermore, it appears that switching between these two states is periodic / time-based
5. If you see flicker, glitching, or HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ errors, switch to FLICKER_TROUBLESHOOTING.pdf.

Score each branch
Question				How to score								Result	
How often did EAC flicker?		Steady ACTIVE / occasional flicker / constant flicker			Occasional flicker or constant AVAILABLE or constant ACTIVE or constant INHIBITED
What error codes appeared?		List from the event log: e.g. HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ x4, NONE rest		
Wheel motion feel			Smooth ramps / smooth with kinks / jerky throughout			
Big angle (30) reached without fault?	Yes / No / Partial (got stuck at X deg)					
Sign convention still right?		Positive = right (sanity check)						

Validation Checklist
Result		Check					Pass Criterion
Fail		Engage at 0 deg				EAC reaches ACTIVE within 2 seconds, holds steady for 10 seconds
Fail		Slide to +5 and back			No HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ in event log. EAC stays ACTIVE the whole time.
Fail		Slide to +30 and back			Same. Wheel motion is visually smooth.
Fail		Slide to -30 and back			Same. Sign convention still positive=right.
Pass		E-STOP via ESC mid-motion		Wheel stops within ~200 ms. Status reads E-STOP.
Pass		Reconnect after E-STOP			DISCONNECT then CONNECT works. ENGAGE works without restart.
Pass		Bus Errors counter			Stays at 0 throughout the entire session.
Fail		Divergence (commanded vs measured)	< 2 deg in steady state. < 5 deg during ramps.
Fail		0x370 RX rate				> 80 frames/sec the whole time.
CY: 0x370 rate appears to be ~20 fps, I question if this criterion is correct.

2. Theory A

CY 1. Run can_sniffer.py first. Confirm "TESLA CHASSIS CAN CONFIRMED" and turn the wheel by hand to verify the angle decode tracks. This proves
the bus is alive AND that 0x370 is being read correctly.
CY 2. Run move.py 0. Just engage at center. Wheel should not move. eacStatus should transition to ACTIVE. If it does, the protocol is healthy.
3. Run move.py 5, then move.py -5, then move.py 0. Confirm sign convention matches what Jordan saw on 03 May (positive = right).
move 5: did not observe motion, but eventually made it to 5
retest move 5: works well, move -5: got stuck but eventually made it, move 0: works well
CY 4. Use steer.py for live control or tesla_steering_test.py for slider control.
Same flickering behavior as before
CY: something we noticed. ALl of these tests have had the comma 3x plugged in. Last test, once we unplugged the comma3x,
it got stuck in INHIBITED, that was probably the "weird" error. We repeated that today and it appears directly correlated.
When the comma 3x is unplugged, it gets stuck in INHIBITED and errors to OUT_OF_RANGE. When the comma 3x boots up and
gets to the screen with the camera, it stops being INHIBITED. Will list under LOG_1 ending at 21:14
JH: unplugged the steerinng rack from the rest of the CAN bus. This immediately stopped all CAN activity confirmed by can_sniffer
5. If you see flicker, glitching, or HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ errors, switch to FLICKER_TROUBLESHOOTING.pdf.

Score each branch
Question				How to score								Result	
How often did EAC flicker?		Steady ACTIVE / occasional flicker / constant flicker			Same as main branch with occasional ACTIVE states being longer-lived
What error codes appeared?		List from the event log: e.g. HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ x4, NONE rest		
Wheel motion feel			Smooth ramps / smooth with kinks / jerky throughout			Same as main branch
Big angle (30) reached without fault?	Yes / No / Partial (got stuck at X deg)					No
Sign convention still right?		Positive = right (sanity check)						Yes

Validation Checklist
Result		Check					Pass Criterion
Fail		Engage at 0 deg				EAC reaches ACTIVE within 2 seconds, holds steady for 10 seconds
Fail		Slide to +5 and back			No HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ in event log. EAC stays ACTIVE the whole time.
Fail		Slide to +30 and back			Same. Wheel motion is visually smooth.
Fail		Slide to -30 and back			Same. Sign convention still positive=right.
Pass		E-STOP via ESC mid-motion		Wheel stops within ~200 ms. Status reads E-STOP.
Pass		Reconnect after E-STOP			DISCONNECT then CONNECT works. ENGAGE works without restart.
Pass		Bus Errors counter			Stays at 0 throughout the entire session.
Fail		Divergence (commanded vs measured)	< 2 deg in steady state. < 5 deg during ramps.
Fail		0x370 RX rate				> 80 frames/sec the whole time.

3. Theory B

CY 1. Run can_sniffer.py first. Confirm "TESLA CHASSIS CAN CONFIRMED" and turn the wheel by hand to verify the angle decode tracks. This proves
the bus is alive AND that 0x370 is being read correctly.
CY 2. Run move.py 0. Just engage at center. Wheel should not move. eacStatus should transition to ACTIVE. If it does, the protocol is healthy.
CY 3. Run move.py 5, then move.py -5, then move.py 0. Confirm sign convention matches what Jordan saw on 03 May (positive = right).
Takes a while for it to start turning, same behavior as before
4. Use steer.py for live control or tesla_steering_test.py for slider control.
5. If you see flicker, glitching, or HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ errors, switch to FLICKER_TROUBLESHOOTING.pdf.

Score each branch
Question				How to score								Result	
How often did EAC flicker?		Steady ACTIVE / occasional flicker / constant flicker			
What error codes appeared?		List from the event log: e.g. HIGH_ANGLE_RATE_REQ x4, NONE rest		
Wheel motion feel			Smooth ramps / smooth with kinks / jerky throughout			
Big angle (30) reached without fault?	Yes / No / Partial (got stuck at X deg)					
Sign convention still right?		Positive = right (sanity check)						

Validation Checklist
Result		Check					Pass Criterion
Fail		Engage at 0 deg				EAC reaches ACTIVE within 2 seconds, holds steady for 10 seconds

Overall comments:
1. I enjoyed the last test plan. It doesn't have to be a full script, it can be more like a table like this document, but it should be in a text document so I can write in it. This time there were many documents, and it was hard to follow a procedure. One cenntralized document would be better.
2. To be clear, thee flickering is not a direct result of a steering commannd being sent. It happenns regardless of divergence. It seems periodic in nature and it seems to have a few states, where it can flicker between INHIBITED/AVAILABLE, AVAILABLE/ACTIVE, or it could be static on INHIBITED, AVAILABLE, or ACTIVE. Sometimes these states are accompanied by a specific ERROR, maybe you can analyze the logs for that.
3. None of the branches fixed the flickering issuee, it was really the same issue for each branch. I did notice some differences in the rate of change of commanded, but I did not write anything down about that.
4. To connfirm I am actually running the new branches, I copied line 81 of the program: # THEORY B: rate limiter creates a discontinuity that trips the rack
5. We found out that something about the comma 3x connection to the CAN bus is affecting our program, it is directly correlated.
6. You should ask us more details about our setup if you thinnk they will be useful. Also, try to think outside the box more for solutions. Software is good to focus on, but it could be hardware related as well.
7. Jordan wonnders if using a Red Panda inn our setup could help anywhere. I don't see how but I thought I would suggest it to facilitate brainstorming.
8. We do not have our CAN device connected to the OBD-II port. It is connected to X437 AKA TDC, located under the center screen. It is on the same CAN bus as pins 1 and 9 of the OBD-II connector.
